❯ Rewrite README.md as the front page of a well-documented open-source project. 
  A reviewer should understand what the service does, how it works and how to   
  run it without opening any other file; deeper detail lives in docs/ and is    
  linked. Derive everything from the code, specs, ADRs and Terraform in this    
  repository: every endpoint, table, module, header, environment variable and   
  command you mention must exist, and nothing may be invented.                  
                                                                                
  Structure:                                                                    
  1. Title, a one-paragraph summary, and badges: CI status, Node version and    
  TypeScript.                                                                   
  2. Table of contents.                                                         
  2b. A reviewer's guide: a 15-minute path for someone who will only read the   
  repository: run it with one command, the five money guarantees and the test   
  that proves each one, the three ADRs to read first (modular monolith,         
  concurrency, idempotency), where the traceability report is, and where the AI 
  usage log and my own decisions are.                                           
  3. Highlights: the guarantees (no lost or duplicated money, no negative       
  customer balance, safe retries, safe concurrency across replicas, a full      
  audit trail), each with one sentence on how it is enforced and a link to the  
  test that proves it.                                                          
  4. Quickstart with only Docker installed: clone, docker compose up --build,   
  make seed, make token, then a deposit, a transfer and a balance query with    
  curl, showing the real responses. Then replay the same request with the same  
  Idempotency-Key and show the Idempotent-Replayed header.                      
  5. How it works: the diagrams listed below, each with a one-line title and    
  two or three sentences saying what to look at.                                
  6. API overview: a table of every endpoint (method, path, role,               
  Idempotency-Key required, success status), the error format with one real     
  example, and links to docs/api/README.md, docs/api/openapi.yaml and the       
  Swagger UI at /docs.                                                          
  7. Configuration: a table of every environment variable (name, default,       
  purpose), taken from the config schema.                                       
  8. Testing: the test levels (unit, integration, e2e, load) and what each one  
  proves, how to run each one, the traceability report, and the latest numbers  
  from docs/performance.md.                                                     
  8b. CI and quality gates, read from .github/workflows/ci.yml: a table with    
  every job and each of its steps, what it checks and what makes it fail        
  (typecheck, lint, format and unit tests; OpenAPI lint; terraform fmt,         
  validate, tflint and checkov with the policies of infra/policies; integration 
  tests and the reconcile after them; the trace gate per level; the build; the  
  e2e suite with the load test at LOAD_RATE_PER_SECOND 100 in CI and why;       
  traceability over the three reports; gitleaks over the full history; npm      
  audit and trivy fs, config and image; and the docs job this phase adds for    
  the Mermaid diagrams and relative links). Then when CI runs (pull requests to 
  main, pushes to main, on demand), the supply-chain controls (actions pinned   
  to commit SHAs, images and tools pinned by digest, a read-only token, runners 
  pinned to ubuntu-24.04, Dependabot without major updates), the gates /ship    
  runs locally before every push, and the command that runs each check locally. 
  Pair it with diagram k.                                                       
  9. Observability and operations: logs and correlation ids, metrics, health    
  checks, graceful shutdown, the Grafana dashboards and the optional error      
  reporting of phase 12b (how to open them with the compose profile), and links 
  to the runbooks.                                                              
  10. Security: a summary of the controls, linking docs/security.md.            
  11. Deployment to AWS: a summary, the diagram, and links to                   
  docs/deployment/aws.md and infra/terraform.                                   
  12. Design decisions: a table (decision, main alternative, why, ADR link)     
  covering every ADR.                                                           
  13. Project structure: an annotated tree of the repository.                   
  14. Development workflow: spec-first, ADRs, the AI working agreement, /audit  
  and /ship, Conventional Commits.                                              
  15. Limitations and roadmap, linking docs/limitations.md.                     
  16. How this was built: for now only a link to the AI usage log in docs/ai/;  
  I add my own text at the end of this phase. Write no TODO marker in any file. 
  17. Troubleshooting: what a newcomer is likely to hit (ports in use, the      
  Docker Compose plugin on macOS, a stale volume after a schema change) and how 
  to fix it.                                                                    
                                                                                
  Diagrams, in Mermaid inside the README so GitHub renders them. Use only       
  flowchart, sequenceDiagram, erDiagram and stateDiagram-v2; no C4 or beta      
  diagram types, which GitHub may not render. Keep labels short and readable.   
  a. System context: clients, load balancer, service replicas, PostgreSQL,      
  Redis, and what is outside the system (payment rails, identity provider).     
  b. Local stack: the compose services (nginx, the two api replicas, migrate,   
  postgres, redis, tools, and prometheus and grafana of the profile             
  observability, drawn as optional), their ports, and which talks to which.     
  c. Inside the service: the hexagonal layers of each module (adapters,         
  application, domain) and the platform layer, with dependencies pointing       
  inward.                                                                       
  d. Request lifecycle: nginx, then the checks in the order of SYS-R31          
  (correlation id, auth, rate limit, role, malformed request, idempotent replay 
  before validation), the use case, the unit of work, PostgreSQL, and           
  problem+json on errors.                                                       
  e. Data model: an ER diagram of every table, with keys and the main           
  constraints noted.                                                            
  f. Transfer, happy path: a sequence diagram with the idempotency key insert,  
  the account locks in id order, the balance checks after the locks, the ledger 
  entries, the balance updates, the audit record, the stored response and the   
  commit.                                                                       
  g. The same request on two replicas at once: a sequence diagram showing the   
  second one waiting on the key row and then receiving the stored response.     
  h. Crossed transfers A→B and B→A at the same time: a sequence diagram showing 
  why locking in id order prevents a deadlock.                                  
  i. Account lifecycle: a state diagram of active, frozen and closed, with the  
  allowed transitions and the role that can trigger each one.                   
  j. AWS deployment: the VPC with public and private subnets across two         
  availability zones, ALB with WAF, ECS tasks, RDS Proxy, RDS Multi-AZ,         
  ElastiCache, Secrets Manager and CloudWatch.                                  
  k. CI pipeline: the jobs and what each one gates.                             
  l. Development workflow: spec, ADR, plan, tests, code, /audit, /ship.         
                                                                                
  Keep sections short and scannable, with tables and diagrams instead of long   
  paragraphs. Run every Quickstart command against the running stack and paste  
  the real outputs.                                                             
  Some of the documents to link are written in the next prompts of this phase.  
  Link them by their final paths now: docs/README.md, docs/architecture.md,     
  docs/api/README.md, docs/api/requests.http, docs/observability.md,            
  docs/security.md, docs/deployment/aws.md, docs/development.md,                
  docs/limitations.md, docs/runbooks/README.md and CHANGELOG.md.

          + by version and digest, and every relative target of a Markdow
          +n link or link reference definition outside code blocks and co
          +de spans names a file or folder of the repository, with its `#
          +` anchor, when the target is a Markdown file, matching a headi
          +ng of that file under GitHub's anchor rules; `http:`, `https:`
          + and `mailto:` targets are not checked                        
      505 +                                                              
      506  ## 4. Error catalogue
      507  
      508  Errors shared by every capability are in spec 000, and the loa
           d balancer's 429 and 413 in spec 007. This spec adds:
     ...
      533  - A custom domain and its DNS records; the certificate's domai
           n is a Terraform variable.
      534  - Hot reload and source mounts for development inside Compose;
            development runs on the host with `npm run dev`.
      535  - Prometheus and Grafana in AWS: scraping in AWS stays the nex
           t step that `docs/deployment/aws.md` documents. Locally: alert
            rules, log or trace backends (Loki, Tempo), and keeping metri
           cs after the container is removed.
      536 +- Checking external URLs in the documentation: a link to anoth
          +er site can break or change without a commit, so `npm run docs
          +:check` checks only relative links (section 1.10).            
      537  - Error reporting in AWS (section 1.10 of spec 007): the tasks
            have no outbound internet path (section 1.7), so the Terrafor
           m sets no `SENTRY_DSN` and reporting stays off; enabling it ne
           eds a NAT gateway with an egress allow-list for the endpoint, 
           and the DSN as a Secrets Manager secret.
      538

        1  # SuperCool Finances
        2  
        3 -Balance service for SuperCool Finances: customer accounts, a d
          -ouble-entry ledger and money movements.                       
        4 -                                                              
        3  [![CI](https://github.com/torsello/supercool-finances/actions/
           workflows/ci.yml/badge.svg)](https://github.com/torsello/super
           cool-finances/actions/workflows/ci.yml)
        4 +[![Node 24](https://img.shields.io/badge/node-24_LTS-339933?lo
          +go=node.js&logoColor=white)](.nvmrc)                          
        5 +[![TypeScript 6 strict](https://img.shields.io/badge/TypeScrip
          +t-6_strict-3178C6?logo=typescript&logoColor=white)](tsconfig.j
          +son)                                                          
        6  
        7 -## Quick start                                                
        7 +A balance service for SuperCool Finances. It provides customer
          + accounts in five currencies, a double-entry ledger, deposits,
          + withdrawals, transfers and reversals, behind a versioned HTTP
          + API. It is a modular monolith in TypeScript on Fastify. Postg
          +reSQL is its only source of truth and Redis is used for rate l
          +imiting. It runs as several replicas behind a load balancer an
          +d stays correct under concurrent and repeated requests. Every 
          +behaviour is specified before it is built, and every acceptanc
          +e criterion is proven by a test that names it. It was built fo
          +r the take-home challenge in [docs/challenge.md](docs/challeng
          +e.md).                                                        
        8  
        9 -### Run it with Docker only                                   
        9 +## Contents                                                   
       10  
       11 -Prerequisite: Docker Engine 24 or later with Compose v2.20 or 
          -later. No Node, no `.env` file: `compose.yaml` holds visibly f
          -ake demo values, which the service refuses when `NODE_ENV` is 
          -`production`.                                                 
       11 +- [Reviewer's guide](#reviewers-guide)                        
       12 +- [Highlights](#highlights)                                   
       13 +- [Quickstart](#quickstart)                                   
       14 +- [How it works](#how-it-works)                               
       15 +- [API overview](#api-overview)

       29 +## Reviewer's guide                                           
       30 +                                                              
       31 +A 15-minute path through the repository.                      
       32 +                                                              
       33 +1. **Run it** (2 min, Docker only): `docker compose up --build
          + --wait`, then follow the [Quickstart](#quickstart).          
       34 +2. **Check the five money guarantees** (5 min): each has one t
          +est that proves it.                                           
       35 +                                                              
       36 +   | Guarantee                    | Proven by                 
          +                                                              
          +                                                        |     
       37 +   | ---------------------------- | --------------------------
          +--------------------------------------------------------------
          +------------------------------------------------------- |     
       38 +   | No lost or duplicated money  | `DEP-AC11` in [test/e2e/re
          +plica-loss.test.ts](test/e2e/replica-loss.test.ts): a replica 
          +is killed during traffic and the ledger still reconciles |    
       39 +   | No negative customer balance | `MOV-AC13` in [test/integr
          +ation/movements/concurrency.test.ts](test/integration/movement
          +s/concurrency.test.ts): 100 concurrent withdrawals      |     
       40 +   | Safe retries                 | `IDM-AC10` in [test/integr
          +ation/idempotency/concurrent-keys.test.ts](test/integration/id
          +empotency/concurrent-keys.test.ts): one key, two replicas |   
       41 +   | Safe concurrency             | `MOV-AC14` in [test/integr
          +ation/movements/concurrency.test.ts](test/integration/movement
          +s/concurrency.test.ts): crossed and circular transfers |      
       42 +   | Full audit trail             | `MOV-AC16` in [test/integr
          +ation/movements/audit.test.ts](test/integration/movements/audi
          +t.test.ts) and `LED-AC11` in [test/integration/ledger/append-o
          +nly.test.ts](test/integration/ledger/append-only.test.ts) |   
       43 +                                                              
       44 +3. **Read three ADRs** (5 min): [ADR-0002 Modular monolith](do
          +cs/adr/0002-modular-monolith.md), [ADR-0008 Concurrency contro
          +l](docs/adr/0008-read-committed-with-ordered-pessimistic-row-l
          +ocks.md) and [ADR-0009 Idempotency](docs/adr/0009-idempotency-
          +inside-the-movements-transaction.md).                         
       45 +4. **Skim the proof** (2 min): [docs/traceability.md](docs/tra
          +ceability.md) lists every acceptance criterion with the test t
          +hat proves it. CI regenerates it from the test reports and fai
          +ls when the committed copy is stale.                          
       46 +5. **See how AI was used** (1 min): [docs/ai/](docs/ai/README.
          +md) has every session transcript. [docs/ai/00-planning.md](doc
          +s/ai/00-planning.md) holds my planning and key decisions, and 
          +the "Open questions" tables of the [specs](specs/README.md) re
          +cord each decision I made, with its date.                     
       47 +                                                              
       48 +## Highlights                                                 
       49 +                                                              
       50 +| Guarantee                         | How it is enforced      
          +                                                              
          +                                                              
          +                                                              
          +                                                              
          +                          | Proof                             
          +                                                              
          +                                                              
          +                                                |             
       51 +| --------------------------------- | ------------------------
          +--------------------------------------------------------------
          +--------------------------------------------------------------
          +--------------------------------------------------------------
          +--------------------------------------------------------------
          +------------- | ----------------------------------------------
          +--------------------------------------------------------------
          +--------------------------------------------------------------
          +----------------------------------- |                         
       52 +| **No lost or duplicated money**   | Each movement is one dat
          +abase transaction: ledger entries, balance changes, audit reco
          +rd and stored response commit together or not at all. A deferr
          +ed trigger refuses any transaction whose entries do not sum to
          + zero.                                                        
          +                 | [DEP-AC11](test/e2e/replica-loss.test.ts), 
          +[MOV-AC17](test/integration/movements/atomicity.test.ts), [SYS
          +-AC11](test/integration/overview/ledger-invariants.test.ts)   
          +                                      |                       
       53 +| **No negative customer balance**  | Funds are checked only a
          +fter the account row is locked with `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`, a
          +nd the column has `CHECK (balance >= 0)` as a last line of def
          +ence.                                                         
          +                                                              
          +                | [MOV-AC13](test/integration/movements/concur
          +rency.test.ts), [LED-AC08](test/integration/ledger/database-ch
          +ecks.test.ts)                                                 
          +                                       |                      
       54 +| **Safe retries**                  | Every money-moving `POST
          +` requires an `Idempotency-Key`. The key row is the first writ
          +e of the movement's transaction, so a repeat on any replica wa
          +its for the first request, then gets its stored response with 
          +`Idempotent-Replayed: true`.                                  
          +                  | [IDM-AC10](test/integration/idempotency/co
          +ncurrent-keys.test.ts), [SYS-AC14](test/e2e/replicas.test.ts) 
          +                                                              
          +                                        |                     
       55 +| **Safe concurrency across replicas** | READ COMMITTED with c
          +ustomer accounts locked one by one in ascending id order, so c
          +rossed transfers queue instead of deadlocking. A deadlock or s
          +erialization failure is retried at most 3 times. No state live
          +s in a process.                                               
          +                          | [MOV-AC14](test/integration/moveme
          +nts/concurrency.test.ts), [SYS-AC14](test/e2e/replicas.test.ts
          +)                                                             
          +                                                |             
       56 +| **Full audit trail**              | The ledger is append-onl
          +y, enforced by triggers for both database roles, so mistakes a
          +re corrected only by reversals. Every committed movement and s
          +tatus change writes one audit record with the actor, the role 
          +and the correlation id.                                       
          +                  | [MOV-AC16](test/integration/movements/audi
          +t.test.ts), [LED-AC11](test/integration/ledger/append-only.tes
          +t.ts)                                                         
          +                                         |

          + service refuses when `NODE_ENV` is `production`. The `make` t
          +argets wrap `docker compose`; the equivalent commands are in [
          +AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md#5-commands).

       40 -The stack's network is `10.210.0.0/24`, with nginx at the fixe
          -d address `10.210.0.10`, the only one the replicas trust for `

          -0.0.12`. If another network of the host already uses that rang
          -e, `docker compose up` fails with `invalid pool request: Pool 
          -overlaps with other one on this address space`. Then set `SCF_
          -SUBNET_PREFIX` to three other octets, for example `SCF_SUBNET_
          -PREFIX=10.212.0 docker compose up --build --wait` (and the sam
          -e variable for every later command of that stack, or once in a
          - `.env` file); it moves the subnet, the fixed addresses and th
          -e trusted address together.

       58 -The end-to-end suite and the load test run against the Docker 
          -Compose stack through nginx, at `E2E_BASE_URL`: `http://localh
          -ost:8080` by default, and local only (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`
          - or `[::1]`, any port), since both read the stack's database o
          -n `127.0.0.1` too. `npm run test:e2e` (or `make e2e`) builds a
          -nd starts its own stack under the Compose project `scf-e2e`, f
          -rom empty volumes, on the network `10.211.0.0/24` (`E2E_SUBNET
          -_PREFIX` moves it, as `SCF_SUBNET_PREFIX` does for your stack)
          -, and removes it at the end (`E2E_KEEP_STACK=1` keeps it runni
          -ng). It uses the same host ports, so stop your own stack first
          - with `docker compose down` or `npm run infra:down`. `npm run 
          -load` (or `make load`) runs the load test of SYS-R20 against a
          - running stack: `LOAD_RATE_PER_SECOND` movements per second, 2
          -00 by default, for 60 s over 1000 funded account pairs. It wri
          -tes the result to [`docs/performance.md`](docs/performance.md)
          -, which keeps a local run at 200; the CI job `e2e` runs it at 
          -100, since its runner has 2 CPUs for the whole stack and the g
          -enerator.

          +or`.

      163 +## How it works                                               
      164  
       62 -## API                                                        
      165 +### System context                                            
      166  
       64 -Every endpoint is served under `/v1` and needs `Authorization:
          - Bearer <JWT>` with the role `customer` or `operator` (`npm ru
          -n token -- --sub <uuid> --role customer` prints one for local 
          -use). Swagger UI is at `/docs` and the OpenAPI document at `/d
          -ocs/json`, without credentials; the same document is committed
          - as [`docs/api/openapi.yaml`](docs/api/openapi.yaml), regenera
          -ted with `npm run openapi:export` and linted with `npm run ope
          -napi:lint`. The committed file is generated with the default s
          -ettings, while `/docs/json` shows the running values of `MAX_A
          -MOUNT_MINOR` and `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_TTL_SECONDS`. Every error is
          - `application/problem+json` (RFC 9457), and every response car
          -ries an `X-Request-Id`.                                       
      167 +What the system owns and what stays outside it.               
      168  
       66 -## Authentication                                             
      169 +The service owns customer balances and the ledger, and Postgre
          +SQL holds all of that state. Redis holds only the per-user rat
          +e-limit counters. Payment rails and a real identity provider a
          +re outside the system: deposits and withdrawals move money aga
          +inst one settlement account per currency, and tokens are signe
          +d by a local script ([ADR-0012](docs/adr/0012-simulated-authen
          +tication-with-jwt-and-two-roles.md)).                         
      170  
       68 -Every `/v1` request carries `Authorization: Bearer <JWT>`. The
          - token is signed with HS256 and `JWT_SECRET`, and carries `sub
          -` (the user's id, a UUID), `role` (`customer` or `operator`), 
          -`iat`, `exp` at most 15 minutes later, and the `iss` and `aud`
          - of `JWT_ISSUER` and `JWT_AUDIENCE`. Each replica verifies it 
          -from the token and its configuration alone, so every replica a
          -ccepts the same tokens. A missing, expired or otherwise invali
          -d token answers 401 `/problems/unauthenticated`, and the answe
          -r never says which check failed. The authentication is simulat
          -ed: the service issues no tokens, and a real identity provider
          - would replace the shared secret (ADR-0012).                  
      171 +```mermaid                                                    
      172 +flowchart LR                                                  
      173 +  client["Clients<br/>customers and operators"] -->|HTTPS + JW
          +T| lb["Load balancer<br/>nginx locally, ALB in AWS"]          
      174 +  lb --> api1["API replica"]                                  
      175 +  lb --> api2["API replica"]                                  
      176 +  api1 & api2 -->|ledger, balances, keys, audit| pg[("PostgreS
          +QL<br/>source of truth")]                                     
      177 +  api1 & api2 -->|per-user rate limit| redis[("Redis")]       
      178 +  subgraph outside["Outside the system"]                      
      179 +    idp["Identity provider<br/>(simulated: npm run token)"]   
      180 +    rails["Payment rails<br/>(settlement accounts)"]          
      181 +  end                                                         
      182 +  idp -.issues tokens.-> client                               
      183 +  rails -.funds in and out.-> pg                              
      184 +```                                                           
      185  
       70 -The role decides what a caller may do. Customers open, list an
          -d read their own accounts, read their history and transactions
          -, withdraw and transfer. Operators deposit, read any customer 
          -account or transaction, freeze, unfreeze and close accounts, a
          -nd reverse transactions. An operation the role does not have a
          -nswers 403 `/problems/forbidden`. Another customer's account o
          -r transaction answers 404 `/problems/not-found`, exactly as an
          - unknown id does. The full matrix is table 1.3 of [`specs/006-
          -auth`](specs/006-auth/spec.md), and the e2e suite checks every
          - cell on both replicas.                                       
      186 +### Local stack                                               
      187  
       72 -To mint a token for local use and demos, valid for 15 minutes 
          -and signed with the variables of the environment or `.env`:   
      188 +The services of [compose.yaml](compose.yaml) and the host port
          +s they publish.                                               
      189  
       74 -```sh                                                         
       75 -npm run token -- --sub 0192f0a0-0000-7000-8000-00000000d0c1 --
          -role customer                                                 
       76 -# against the Docker Compose stack, with its demo secret and o
          -nly Docker on the host:                                       
       77 -docker compose run --rm tools npm run --silent token -- --sub 
          -<uuid> --role operator                                        
      190 +Only nginx is meant for clients. The replicas' own ports exist
          + so the tests can reach each replica directly. Every port is b
          +ound to `127.0.0.1`, and `METRICS_PORT` (9464) is never publis
          +hed. Prometheus and Grafana start only with the `observability
          +` profile, and `tools` only runs on demand.                   
      191 +                                                              
      192 +```mermaid                                                    
      193 +flowchart LR                                                  
      194 +  host(["Host"]) -->|127.0.0.1:8080| nginx

       80 -## Accounts                                                   
      208 +### Inside the service                                        
      209  
       82 -A customer opens accounts in one of USD, MXN, EUR, COP or JPY 
          -(`POST /v1/accounts`), lists their own (`GET /v1/accounts`) an
          -d reads each one with its balance and history (`GET /v1/accoun
          -ts/{id}`, `GET /v1/accounts/{id}/entries`). An operator reads 
          -any customer account by id, with its `ownerId`, but lists none
          -. Another customer's account, a system account and an unknown 
          -id all answer the same 404.                                   
      210 +Each module of `src/modules/` (accounts, ledger, movements, id
          +empotency, auth) is a hexagon ([ADR-0003](docs/adr/0003-hexago
          +nal-architecture-with-tactical-ddd.md)).                      
      211  
       84 -Lists go newest first, `limit` from 1 to 100 (20 by default), 
          -and continue with the opaque `nextCursor`, which is signed wit
          -h `CURSOR_SECRET`, accepted by every replica, and valid only f
          -or the list and user it was issued to.                        
      212 +Dependencies point inward: the domain imports nothing from Fas
          +tify, Kysely, `pg` or ioredis, so the money rules are unit-tes
          +ted without infrastructure. Modules talk to each other only th
          +rough each other's `index.ts`, and [src/app.ts](src/app.ts) wi
          +res everything together.                                      
      213  
       86 -An account is `active`, `frozen` or `closed`. Operators freeze
          -, unfreeze and close accounts (`POST /v1/accounts/{id}/freeze`
          -, `/unfreeze`, `/close`); only an account with balance "0" can
          - be closed, and `closed` is final. A frozen or closed account 
          -takes no deposit, withdrawal or transfer on either side; a rev
          -ersal is still allowed on a frozen account. Spec: [`specs/001-
          -accounts`](specs/001-accounts/spec.md).

       88 -## Ledger                                                     
      229 +### Request lifecycle                                         
      230  
       90 -Every money movement is one transaction of two or more ledger 
          -entries that sum to zero, in one currency, written in the same
          - database transaction as the balance changes. Amounts are inte
          -gers in minor units of the currency: `bigint` in the code, str
          -ings of decimal digits such as `"1050"` (10.50 EUR) in the API
          -, and whole yen for JPY. An entry's amount is signed: positive
          - adds to the account's balance, negative subtracts. A deposit,
          - withdrawal or transfer is at most `MAX_AMOUNT_MINOR` minor un
          -its (100000000000 by default).                                
      231 +The order in which every request is checked; the first failure
          + answers (SYS-R31 in [spec 000](specs/000-overview/spec.md)). 
      232  
       92 -Deposits and withdrawals move money against the settlement acc
          -ount of their currency, a system account with no cached balanc
          -e: its balance is the sum of its entries and may go below zero
          -. A customer balance never does. The ledger is append-only, in
          - the code and in the database; a mistake is corrected by a rev
          -ersal (`POST /v1/transactions/{id}/reversals`), never by an ed
          -it.                                                           
      233 +The authentication, rate-limit and role checks answer before t
          +he body is read. A replay of a completed `Idempotency-Key` is 
          +answered before validation and every later check, so it return
          +s what the first request got even after configuration changes.
          + Every error leaves through one function, `toProblem` in [src/
          +platform/http/error-handler.ts](src/platform/http/error-handle
          +r.ts), as `application/problem+json`.                         
      234  
       94 -`npm run reconcile` checks every cached balance against the le
          -dger and every currency's global sum against zero; see the [re
          -conciliation runbook](docs/runbooks/reconciliation.md). Spec: 
          -[`specs/002-ledger`](specs/002-ledger/spec.md).               
      235 +```mermaid                                                    
      236 +flowchart TD                                                  
      237 +  c([Client]) --> ng["nginx<br/>per-IP limit 429, sets X-Reque
          +st-Id"]                                                       
      238 +  ng --> rid["Correlation id<br/>X-Request-Id kept or generate
          +d"]                                                           
      239 +  rid --> route{"Known route?"} -->|no| e404["404"]           
      240 +  route --> auth{"Valid JWT?"} -->|no| e401["401"]            
      241 +  auth --> rl{"Per-user limit<br/>(Redis)"} -->|over| e429["42
          +9"]                                                           
      242 +  rl --> role{"Role allowed?"} -->|no| e403["403"]            
      243 +  role --> media{"JSON, within 16 KiB?"} -->|no| e415["415 / 4
          +13"]                                                          
      244 +  media --> malformed{"Body, key, cursor<br/>well-formed?"} --
          +>|no| e400["400"]                                             
      245 +  malformed --> key{"Idempotency-Key<br/>seen before?"}       
      246 +  key -->|completed, same body| replay["Stored response<br/>Id
          +empotent-Replayed: true"]                                     
      247 +  key -->|other body / in progress| e422k["422 / 409"]        
      248 +  key -->|new| val{"Valid body?"} -->|no| e422["422"]         
      249 +  val --> uc["Use case"] --> uow["Unit of work<br/>one DB tran
          +saction"] --> pg[("PostgreSQL")]

          +with the key"]                                                
      251 +  pg --> ok["201 / 200"]                                      
      252 +  e404 & e401 & e429 & e403 & e415 & e400 & e422k & e422 & sto
          +red -.-> problem["application/problem+json"]                  
      253 +```

       96 -## Money movements                                            
      255 +### Data model                                                
      256  
       98 -Operators deposit into any customer account (`POST /v1/account
          -s/{id}/deposits`); customers withdraw from their own accounts 
          -(`/withdrawals`) and transfer out of them (`/transfers`). Each
          - answers 201 with `Location: /v1/transactions/{id}`; a withdra
          -wal and a transfer also return the account's new balance. A mo
          -vement in another currency than the account's answers 422 `/pr
          -oblems/currency-mismatch`, and a frozen or closed account 422 
          -`/problems/account-not-active`.                               
      257 +Every table of [migrations/](migrations/).                    
      258  
      100 -**A transfer.** `POST /v1/accounts/{A1}/transfers` with `{"des
          -tinationAccountId": "<B1>", "amount": "1050", "currency": "EUR
          -"}` writes one transaction of two entries, −1050 on A1 and +10
          -50 on B1, and both balance changes, in one database transactio
          -n: all of it happens or none of it. A1 never goes below "0" (4
          -22 `/problems/insufficient-funds`). B1 must be an active custo
          -mer account in the same currency. When B1 is another customer'
          -s, every reason it cannot be credited (unknown, a system accou
          -nt, frozen, closed, another currency, a balance that would pas
          -s the maximum) gets one answer, 422 `/problems/destination-una
          -vailable`, so a transfer reveals nothing about other customers
          -' accounts.                                                   
      259 +`accounts` holds customer accounts with a cached balance, plus
          + one settlement system account per currency, which has none ([
          +ADR-0007](docs/adr/0007-system-accounts-without-a-cached-balan
          +ce.md)). `transactions` and `ledger_entries` are append-only. 
          +Composite foreign keys on `(id, currency)` keep each entry in 
          +its account's and its transaction's currency. `pgmigrations` i
          +s node-pg-migrate's record of applied migrations.             
      260  
      102 -**Concurrent transfers.** Each movement locks the customer acc
          -ounts it changes with `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`, one by one in a
          -scending id order, and checks balances and statuses only once 
          -it holds them; system accounts are never locked (ADR-0008). Cr
          -ossed transfers (A1 to B1 while B1 sends to A1) and cycles (A1
          - to B1 to Z1 to A1) take their locks in the same order, so the
          -y queue instead of deadlocking. A deadlock or serialization fa
          -ilure that still happens is retried, up to 3 attempts with a s
          -hort random backoff; an account lock not acquired within `ACCO
          -UNT_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS` (2000 ms) answers 503 `/problems/service-
          -unavailable` with `Retry-After: 1`, and the request can be ret
          -ried with the same `Idempotency-Key`. Spec: [`specs/003-money-
          -movements`](specs/003-money-movements/spec.md).               
      261 +```mermaid                                                    
      262 +erDiagram                                                     
      263 +  accounts ||--o{ ledger_entries : "has"                      
      264 +  transactions ||--|{ ledger_entries : "2 or more, sum 0"     
      265 +  transactions |o--o| transactions : "reversed by (at most onc
          +e)"                                                           
      266 +  transactions |o--o{ audit_records : "recorded in"           
      267 +  accounts {                                                  
      268 +    uuid id PK                                                
      269 +    text kind "customer or system"                            
      270 +    text code UK "system only"                                
      271 +    uuid owner_id "customer only"                             
      272 +    char currency "USD MXN EUR COP JPY"                       
      273 +    text status "active frozen closed"                        
      274 +    bigint balance "customer only, >= 0"                      
      275 +  }                                                           
      276 +  transactions {                                              
      277 +    uuid id PK                                                
      278 +    text kind "deposit withdrawal transfer reversal"          
      279 +    char currency                                             
      280 +    uuid reversed_transaction_id FK,UK "reversals only"       
      281 +    timestamptz created_at                                    
      282 +  }                                                           
      283 +  ledger_entries {                                            
      284 +    uuid id PK                                                
      285 +    uuid transaction_id FK "with currency"                    
      286 +    uuid account_id FK "with currency"                        
      287 +    bigint amount "signed, never 0"                           
      288 +    char currency                                             
      289 +  }                                                           
      290 +  audit_records {                                             
      291 +    uuid id PK                                                
      292 +    uuid actor_id                                             
      293 +    text actor_role                                           
      294 +    text action                                               
      295 +    uuid_array account_ids                                    
      296 +    uuid transaction_id FK                                    
      297 +    uuid reversed_transaction_id FK                           
      298 +    text request_id "correlation id"                          
      299 +  }                                                           
      300 +  idempotency_keys {                                          
      301 +    uuid user_id PK                                           
      302 +    text key PK                                               
      303 +    char fingerprint "sha-256 hex"                            
      304 +    smallint status "stored response"                         
      305 +    bytea body "exact bytes"                                  
      306 +    timestamptz expires_at                                    
      307 +  }                                                           
      308 +  pgmigrations {                                              
      309 +    int id PK                                                 
      310 +    text name                                                 
      311 +    timestamp run_on                                          
      312 +  }                                                           
      313 +```

      104 -## Corrections                                                
      315 +### Transfer, happy path                                      
      316  
      106 -The ledger is never edited. An operator corrects a movement by
          - reversing it: `POST /v1/transactions/{id}/reversals` with a `
          -reason` of 3 to 500 characters, which is stored and audited bu
          -t never returned. The reversal is a new transaction, linked to
          - the original, with every entry of the original negated, and t
          -he balances change with it.                                   
      317 +The statements of one transfer, all in one database transactio
          +n (section 6.2 of [plan 000](specs/000-overview/plan.md)).    
      318  
      108 -A transaction is reversed at most once (409 `/problems/already
          --reversed`), even when two operators try at the same time, and
          - a reversal cannot itself be reversed (422 `/problems/transact
          -ion-not-reversible`). A reversal that would take a customer ba
          -lance below "0", because the money has moved on, answers 422 `
          -/problems/insufficient-funds-for-reversal`. A reversal is allo
          -wed on a frozen account, so a mistake can be undone while the 
          -account is under review, and refused on a closed one (422 `/pr
          -oblems/account-not-active`). Spec: [`specs/004-reversals`](spe
          -cs/004-reversals/spec.md).                                    
      319 +The key row is the first write, so a duplicate waits on it. Th
          +e accounts are locked in ascending id order and checked only o
          +nce the locks are held. The response is stored with the key be
          +fore the commit, and the deferred triggers then check that the
          + entries sum to zero and that the key row holds its response.

      110 -## Duplicate requests

      323 +  participant C as Client                                     
      324 +  participant API as API replica                              
      325 +  participant PG as PostgreSQL                                
      326 +  C->>API: POST /v1/accounts/A/transfers<br/>Idempotency-Key: 
          +k1

          +thod, path, body

      334 +  API->>PG: SELECT ... FOR UPDATE (higher id)

          +the locks

      343  
      112 -Every deposit, withdrawal, transfer and reversal requires an `
          -Idempotency-Key` header, a fresh value such as a UUID for each
          - new request; account creation takes one optionally. Keys belo
          -ng to the user who sent them.                                 
      344 +### Same request on two replicas                              
      345  
      114 -1. The first request with a key runs, and its response (status
          -, headers and body) is stored with the key in the same databas
          -e transaction as the movement.                                
      115 -2. A repeat with the same key, method, path and body, within `
          -IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_TTL_SECONDS` (24 hours by default), gets the s
          -tored response byte for byte, with `Idempotent-Replayed: true`
          -, and moves no money. This holds on any replica, because the k
          -ey lives in PostgreSQL.                                       
      116 -3. A repeat that arrives while the first is still running wait
          -s for it, at most `IDEMPOTENCY_WAIT_TIMEOUT_MS` (2000 ms), and
          - then gets the stored response, or 409 `/problems/request-in-p
          -rogress` with `Retry-After: 1` if the first is still running. 
      117 -4. The same key with another method, path or body answers 422 
          -`/problems/idempotency-key-reused`.                           
      346 +A client retries on another replica while the first attempt is
          + still running.                                               
      347  
      119 -A client that gets a connection error, a 502, 503 or 504, or a
          - 409 `/problems/request-in-progress` retries the same request 
          -with the same key, waiting `Retry-After` (200 ms without one),
          - up to 60 times; it never retries any other 4xx. Expired keys 
          -are deleted by `npm run idempotency:cleanup`; see the [idempot
          -ency cleanup runbook](docs/runbooks/idempotency-cleanup.md). S
          -pec: [`specs/005-idempotency`](specs/005-idempotency/spec.md).
      348 +The second insert of the same key blocks on the first request'
          +s uncommitted row, for at most `IDEMPOTENCY_WAIT_TIMEOUT_MS`. 
          +When the first commits, the second finds the stored response a
          +nd replays it. If the first is still running when the wait end
          +s, the second answers 409 `/problems/request-in-progress` with
          + `Retry-After: 1`.

      136 -Every error is `application/problem+json` (RFC 9457) with `typ

          --0016](docs/adr/0016-error-model.md)). `title` and `detail` ar
          -e fixed per type, so two answers of one type differ only in `r
          -equestId`; one 404 body serves every unknown, foreign or syste
          -m resource and every unknown route. No body holds a stack trac
          -e, SQL or an underlying error's message: a 500 is logged with

          - for an existing reversal, so the unique constraint must answe
          -r 409. |                                                      
      149 -| `extra-response-member`           | Adds a member to every n
          -ew response body from the key step on, never to a replay.

          -nnection after the commit, before the answer is written.

      407 +## API overview                                               
      408  
      152 -The app records the seams it attached in `app.testSeams`, and 
          -SYS-AC24 checks that the production app has none.             
      409 +Every endpoint is under `/v1` and needs `Authorization: Bearer
          + <JWT>`. The role table is table 1.3 of [spec 006](specs/006-a
          +uth/spec.md). Another customer's account, a system account and
          + an unknown id all answer the same 404, and a role that is not
          + allowed gets 403 whatever ids the request names.             
      410  
      411 +| Method | Path                                | Role         
          +       | Idempotency-Key | Success |                          
      412 +| ------ | ----------------------------------- | -------------
          +------ | --------------- | ------- |                          
      413 +| POST   | `/v1/accounts`                      | customer     
          +       | optional        | 201     |                          
      414 +| GET    | `/v1/accounts`                      | customer     
          +       | no              | 200     |                          
      415 +| GET    | `/v1/accounts/{id}`                 | customer, ope
          +rator  | no              | 200     |                          
      416 +| GET    | `/v1/accounts/{id}/entries`         | customer, ope
          +rator  | no              | 200     |                          
      417 +| POST   | `/v1/accounts/{id}/freeze`          | operator     
          +       | no              | 200     |                          
      418 +| POST   | `/v1/accounts/{id}/unfreeze`        | operator     
          +       | no              | 200     |                          
      419 +| POST   | `/v1/accounts/{id}/close`           | operator     
          +       | no              | 200     |                          
      420 +| POST   | `/v1/accounts/{id}/deposits`        | operator     
          +       | **required**    | 201     |                          
      421 +| POST   | `/v1/accounts/{id}/withdrawals`     | customer     
          +       | **required**    | 201     |                          
      422 +| POST   | `/v1/accounts/{id}/transfers`       | customer     
          +       | **required**    | 201     |                          
      423 +| GET    | `/v1/transactions/{id}`             | customer, ope
          +rator  | no              | 200     |                          
      424 +| POST   | `/v1/transactions/{id}/reversals`   | operator     
          +       | **required**    | 201     |                          
      425 +                                                              
      426 +Outside `/v1`, without credentials: `GET /health/live`, `GET /
          +health/ready`, Swagger UI at `/docs` and the OpenAPI document 
          +at `/docs/json`. Metrics are served at `/metrics`, but only on
          + `METRICS_PORT`.                                              
      427 +                                                              
      428 +Amounts are strings of decimal digits in minor units (`"1050"`
          + is 10.50 EUR; JPY has no minor unit), in USD, MXN, EUR, COP o
          +r JPY. Lists are newest first, keyset-paginated with an opaque
          + signed `nextCursor` ([ADR-0017](docs/adr/0017-keyset-paginati
          +on-with-signed-cursors.md)). Every response carries `X-Request
          +-Id`. A created movement carries `Location: /v1/transactions/{
          +id}`.                                                         
      429 +                                                              
      430 +**Errors** are `application/problem+json` (RFC 9457, [ADR-0016
          +](docs/adr/0016-error-model.md)). `title` and `detail` are fix
          +ed per type, and no body ever holds a stack trace, SQL or an i
          +nternal message. A real one, from a withdrawal larger than the
          + balance:                                                     
      431 +                                                              
      432 +```http                                                       
      433 +HTTP/1.1 422 Unprocessable Entity                             
      434 +Content-Type: application/problem+json                        
      435 +x-request-id: e9275fb111700546ad82b5b4d1779e25                
      436 +                                                              
      437 +{"type":"/problems/insufficient-funds","title":"Insufficient F
          +unds","status":422,"detail":"The account balance does not cove
          +r the amount.","requestId":"e9275fb111700546ad82b5b4d1779e25"}
      438 +```                                                           
      439 +                                                              
      440 +Every problem type and when it is answered is in section 4 of 
          +[spec 000](specs/000-overview/spec.md). More:                 
      441 +                                                              
      442 +- [docs/api/README.md](docs/api/README.md): a guide to the API
          +.                                                             
      443 +- [docs/api/openapi.yaml](docs/api/openapi.yaml): the OpenAPI 
          +document, generated from the route schemas (`npm run openapi:e
          +xport`) and linted in CI.                                     
      444 +- [docs/api/requests.http](docs/api/requests.http): ready-to-r
          +un requests.                                                  
      445 +- Swagger UI: <http://localhost:8080/docs> on the running stac
          +k.

      158 -- The timeouts must fit inside each other: `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS
          -` (25000 ms) above the worst case of the lock waits, the pool

          - below it (SEC-R35). See the [timeouts runbook](docs/runbooks/
          -timeouts-and-503.md).

          -every secret comes from Secrets Manager, and each database pas
          -sword arrives as `PGPASSWORD` beside a URL that holds none.

          -the URLs and `SENTRY_DSN` are redacted from every log line (SE

      484  
      168 -- `GET /health/live` answers 200 `{"status": "ok"}` while the 
          -process runs, checking nothing else (SEC-R23). The image's `HE
          -ALTHCHECK`, the ECS container health check and the ALB target 
          -group all use it, so a database outage never makes Docker or E
          -CS replace every replica.                                     
      169 -- `GET /health/ready` answers 200 `{"status": "ready"}` only w
          -hen `SELECT 1` completes within 1000 ms on a connection kept a
          -part from the request pool and every migration the code ships 
          -is applied; otherwise 503 `/problems/service-unavailable`, and
          - a `warn` line names the failed check (SEC-R24). It answers 50
          -3 from the start of a shutdown (SEC-R26).

      189 -## Error reporting                                            
      533 +The e2e job runs the load test at 100 requests per second rath
          +er than 200, because its runner has 2 CPUs for the whole stack
          + and the load generator. The p99 is reported, never enforced. 
      534  
      191 -Off by default: the demo and the tests need no key and send no
          -thing outside the machine. With `SENTRY_DSN` set, each replica
          - reports every request answered 500 (`/problems/internal-error
          -`) to that Sentry-compatible endpoint (Sentry, self-hosted Sen
          -try or GlitchTip), written by the service's own client with No
          -de's `fetch` (section 1.10 of [`specs/007-security-ops`](specs
          -/007-security-ops/spec.md), ADR-0023). Nothing else is reporte
          -d: no 4xx, no 503, no error outside a request.                
      535 +**Supply chain.** Every action is pinned to a commit SHA, ever
          +y image and tool by version and digest (or checksum, for gitle
          +aks), and every job runs on `ubuntu-24.04` with a read-only `G
          +ITHUB_TOKEN`. [Dependabot](.github/dependabot.yml) updates npm
          +, Docker, Compose and Actions weekly, and never proposes a maj
          +or version.                                                   
      536  
      193 -A report holds only the correlation id (`requestId`, which fin
          -ds the request's log lines), the route template, the method, t
          -he replica, the SQLSTATE when there is one, the error's type, 
          -its stack frames and its message, scrubbed of secrets, the req
          -uest's token and key, JWT-shaped tokens, UUIDs and every run o
          -f digits. It never holds a header (so neither `Authorization` 
          -nor `Idempotency-Key`), a query string, a body, a client addre
          -ss, an amount, an account id, a token or a secret. Reporting n
          -ever changes or delays an answer: a send is abandoned after 2 
          -s, at most 20 reports wait per replica, and a failure is dropp
          -ed and logged once when sending starts failing and once when i
          -t works again.                                                
      537 +**Before every push**, `/ship` runs `npm run check`, `npm run 
          +test:integration`, `npm run trace -- --require unit,integratio
          +n`, checkov on `infra/terraform` and gitleaks over the new com
          +mits. It never pushes a failing gate.                         
      538  
      195 -`compose.yaml` never passes `SENTRY_DSN`, so a value left in t
          -he shell or a stray `.env` sends nothing. To turn it on for th
          -e local stack, add the override file, which refuses to start w
          -ithout one:                                                   
      539 +**Locally**, each CI step is the same command shown in the tab
          +le. The tools it needs come from Docker images, so Docker and 
          +Node 24 are the only prerequisites.

      541 +## Observability and operations                               
      542 +                                                              
      543 +| Concern               | What there is                       
          +                                                              
          +                                                              
          +                                                              
          +                                      |                       
      544 +| --------------------- | ------------------------------------
          +--------------------------------------------------------------
          +--------------------------------------------------------------
          +--------------------------------------------------------------
          +--------------------------------------- |                     
      545 +| Logs                  | One JSON line per event (pino) with 
          +`reqId` (the correlation id) and `replicaId`. nginx logs JSON 
          +too. Tokens, secrets, `Idempotency-Key` values, passwords and 
          +`SENTRY_DSN` are redacted, and query strings are never logged.
          +                                           |                  
      546 +| Correlation id        | `X-Request-Id`: the client's value w
          +hen it matches `[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,128}`, otherwise generated. 
          +It is returned on every response and stored in every problem b
          +ody and audit record.                                         
          +                                        |                     
      547 +| Metrics               | Prometheus at `/metrics` on `METRICS
          +_PORT`: request latency by route and status, money movements b
          +y kind and outcome, idempotent replays, lock timeouts, transac
          +tion retries, pool usage and rate limiting, plus Node's proces
          +s metrics.                                 |                  
      548 +| Health                | `GET /health/live` checks only the p
          +rocess. `GET /health/ready` checks `SELECT 1` and that every m
          +igration is applied, and answers 503 from the start of a shutd
          +own.                                                          
          +                                          |                   
      549 +| Shutdown              | On SIGTERM: readiness 503, a drain d
          +elay, stop accepting connections, finish in-flight requests wi
          +thin `SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_MS`, close pool and Redis, then exit 0,
          + or 1 if work was cut off.                                    
          +                                          |                   
      550 +| Dashboards (optional) | `make observability` (or `docker com
          +pose --profile observability up --build --wait`) adds Promethe
          +us and Grafana at <http://localhost:3030>, anonymous and read-
          +only. Its panels show replicas, requests and errors, latency, 
          +movements, lock timeouts, replays, rate limiting and the pool.
          + |                                                            
      551 +| Error reports (optional) | Off by default. `SENTRY_DSN=<dsn>
          + docker compose -f compose.yaml -f compose.error-reporting.yam
          +l up --build --wait` reports every 500 to a Sentry-compatible 
          +endpoint, scrubbed of headers, bodies, amounts, ids and secret
          +s ([ADR-0023](docs/adr/0023-optional-observability-off-by-defa
          +ult.md)). |                                                   
      552 +                                                              
      553 +The details are in [docs/observability.md](docs/observability.
          +md). The runbooks are indexed in [docs/runbooks/README.md](doc
          +s/runbooks/README.md): [deploy and migrate](docs/runbooks/depl
          +oy-and-migrate.md), [rate limits](docs/runbooks/rate-limits.md
          +), [timeouts and 503](docs/runbooks/timeouts-and-503.md), [shu
          +tdown](docs/runbooks/shutdown.md), [reconciliation](docs/runbo
          +oks/reconciliation.md) and [idempotency cleanup](docs/runbooks
          +/idempotency-cleanup.md).                                     
      554 +                                                              
      555 +## Security                                                   
      556 +                                                              
      557 +- **Authentication and authorization:** an HS256 JWT on every 
          +`/v1` request (`sub`, `role`, `iss`, `aud`, `exp` at most 15 m
          +inutes ahead). The role is checked on every route, and foreign
          + resources answer 404 like unknown ones.                      
      558 +- **Strict input:** Zod schemas reject unknown fields. The ser
          +vice accepts only `application/json` bodies of at most 16 KiB 
          +(nginx: 32 KiB). SQL is always parameterized, through Kysely o
          +r `pg`.                                                       
      559 +- **Rate limits:** per IP at nginx (or AWS WAF), and per user 
          +in Redis, which fails open after 100 ms ([ADR-0013](docs/adr/0
          +013-rate-limiting-at-the-edge-and-in-redis.md)).              
      560 +- **Least-privilege database:** an owner role runs the migrati
          +ons, and the runtime role may only read and append to the ledg
          +er. Triggers refuse `UPDATE`, `DELETE` and `TRUNCATE` for both
          + roles ([ADR-0018](docs/adr/0018-two-database-roles.md)).     
      561 +- **Headers:** a strict CSP, HSTS, `nosniff`, `X-Frame-Options
          +: DENY`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` and `Cache-Control: no
          +-store` under `/v1`. CORS is off unless `CORS_ORIGINS` lists e
          +xact origins. `X-Forwarded-For` is trusted only from `TRUSTED_
          +PROXY_CIDRS`.                                                 
      562 +- **Secrets:** never logged, demo secrets refused in productio
          +n, and in AWS held only in Secrets Manager. gitleaks scans the
          + whole history in CI.                                         
      563 +                                                              
      564 +The full threat model and controls are in [docs/security.md](d
          +ocs/security.md).                                             
      565 +                                                              
      566 +## Deployment to AWS                                          
      567 +                                                              
      568 +The target architecture is in Terraform under [infra/terraform
          +/](infra/terraform/): seven modules (`network`, `edge`, `servi
          +ce`, `database`, `cache`, `secrets`, `observability`) ([ADR-00
          +14](docs/adr/0014-aws-deployment-on-ecs-fargate-with-rds-postg
          +resql.md), [ADR-0015](docs/adr/0015-terraform-for-infrastructu
          +re-as-code.md)). CI validates and scans it on every change, an
          +d nothing in the repository applies it.

          +t-in-the-domain.md) | Digit strings in the API, `bigint` in th

      214 -- `ci`: `npm run check`, `npm run openapi:lint`, `npm run infr
          -a:validate`, the integration tests against Postgres and Redis,
          - `npm run reconcile` on the test database they leave (LED-AC17
          -), `npm run trace -- --require unit,integration` and `npm run 
          -build`. It uploads the unit and integration reports.          
      215 -- `e2e`: `npm run test:e2e` on its own Compose project, `scf-e
          -2e`, with the load test at `LOAD_RATE_PER_SECOND` 100; it uplo
          -ads the e2e report, and on failure prints the stack's logs and
          - uploads them with `reports/load-test.json`.                  
      216 -- `traceability`: after `ci` and `e2e`, `npm run trace -- --re
          -quire unit,integration,e2e --write` on the three reports, so e
          -very acceptance criterion has a passing test at its level, the
          -n fails if the regenerated [`docs/traceability.md`](docs/trace
          -ability.md) differs from the committed one.                   
      217 -- `secret-scan`: gitleaks over the full git history.          
      218 -- `security`: `npm audit --audit-level=high`, and trivy on the
          - files, on the configuration and on the runtime image, from th
          -e `aquasec/trivy` image pinned by digest, failing on any HIGH 
          -or CRITICAL finding.

      222 -## Runbooks                                                   
      685 +- **Spec first.** No behaviour exists without an acceptance cr
          +iterion in [specs/](specs/README.md). When the code and a spec
          + disagree, the spec wins.                                     
      686 +- **ADRs.** Every decision is recorded in [docs/adr/](docs/adr
          +/README.md), with its alternatives.                           
      687 +- **The AI working agreement.** [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) holds t
          +he money, concurrency, idempotency and security rules every ag
          +ent follows, and the commands.                                
      688 +- **`/audit`** runs a read-only review in a separate agent tha
          +t has not seen the conversation, against the specs, ADRs and A
          +GENTS.md.                                                     
      689 +- **`/ship`** runs the gates, commits with Conventional Commit
          +s that list the ACs covered, pushes the phase branch and opens
          + the pull request. Nothing is committed to `main` directly.

      224 -- [Deploy and migrate](docs/runbooks/deploy-and-migrate.md)   
      225 -- [Rate limits](docs/runbooks/rate-limits.md)

      227 -- [Shutdown](docs/runbooks/shutdown.md)                       
      228 -- [Reconciliation](docs/runbooks/reconciliation.md)           
      229 -- [Idempotency cleanup](docs/runbooks/idempotency-cleanup.md)

          +env:sync`, `npm run infra:up`, `npm run migrate:up`, then `npm
          + run dev`. More is in [docs/development.md](docs/development.m

      692 +                                                              
      693 +## Limitations and roadmap                                    
      694 +                                                              
      695 +The authentication is simulated, there are no payment rails or
          + currency conversion, and Prometheus scraping and error report
          +ing are not wired in AWS. The full list, with what would come 
          +next, is in [docs/limitations.md](docs/limitations.md). Change
          +s are recorded in [CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md).

      701 +## Troubleshooting                                            
      702 +                                                              
      703 +| Symptom                                                     
          +                                    | Fix                     
          +                                                              
          +                                                              
          +                                                              
          +                        |                                     
      704 +| ------------------------------------------------------------
          +----------------------------------- | ------------------------
          +--------------------------------------------------------------
          +--------------------------------------------------------------
          +--------------------------------------------------------------
          +----------------------- |                                     
      705 +| `Bind for 127.0.0.1:8080 failed: port is already allocated` 
          +(or 3001, 3002, 55432, 6379, 3030) | Another process or stack 
          +holds the port. Find it with `lsof -i :8080`, stop it, or stop
          + this project's other stack with `docker compose down`. `npm r
          +un test:e2e` uses the same ports, so stop your stack before it
          +.                         |                                   
      706 +| `docker: 'compose' is not a docker command` on macOS        
          +                                     | The Compose v2 plugin i
          +s missing. Install or update Docker Desktop, or, with Homebrew
          +'s `docker`, run `brew install docker-compose` and add `"cliPl
          +uginsExtraDirs": ["/opt/homebrew/lib/docker/cli-plugins"]` to 
          +`~/.docker/config.json`. |                                    
      707 +| `invalid pool request: Pool overlaps with other one on this 
          +address space`                       | Another Docker network 
          +uses `10.210.0.0/24`. Run with another prefix, for example `SC
          +F_SUBNET_PREFIX=10.212.0 docker compose up --build --wait`, an
          +d pass the same variable to every later command of that stack.
          +                          |                                   
      708 +| `migrate` exits non-zero, or `/health/ready` answers 503 aft
          +er a schema change                   | The database volume pre
          +dates the change, for example an edited migration or init scri
          +pt. Recreate it: `docker compose down -v && docker compose up 
          +--build --wait`; for the host's Postgres, `npm run infra:reset
          +`.                       |                                    
      709 +| `401 /problems/unauthenticated` on every request            
          +                                     | The token expired (15 m
          +inutes) or was minted for another stack's secret. Mint a new o
          +ne with `make token`.                                         
          +                                                              
          +                         |                                    
      710 +| `409 /problems/request-in-progress`                         
          +                                     | The same `Idempotency-K
          +ey` is still running elsewhere. Retry the same request after `
          +Retry-After`.                                                 
          +                                                              
          +                          |

          +md` and `reports/load-test.json`, and exits 1 on a 5xx, a conn
          +ection error, a lost request, a generator behind its schedule 
          +or a ledger that does not reconcile, never on a missed p99.

          +                                                          | Li
          +sts every AC in `specs/` with its proof, read from the Vitest 
          +JSON reports in `reports/` written by `npm test`, `npm run tes
          +t:integration` and `npm run test:e2e`. Fails when an AC of an 
          +`Implemented` spec, or one named by a ticked task in `tasks.md
          +`, has no passing test in the report of its level (level `ci`:
          + a `Verified by` line that names no `npm run <script>`, or nam
          +es one that is not a script in `package.json` or not the whole
          + command, `npm run <script>` with plain arguments at most, of 
          +a step of `.github/workflows/ci.yml` that can fail the build) 
          +or is named by any test that did not pass; when a test or task
          + names an undefined AC; when a project named in `--require` ha
          +s no report; and when a spec or task file is malformed. CI's `
          +ci` job and `/ship` pass `--require unit,integration`, and CI'
          +s `traceability` job, after the `ci` and `e2e` jobs, passes `-
          +-require unit,integration,e2e --write`, so e2e ACs are enforce
          +d in CI from phase 12-infra, and fails when the regenerated `d
          +ocs/traceability.md` differs from the committed one (from phas
          +e 13-final-review). | 02-specs          |

      229  Rolling back the code is applying the previous tag; the schema
